ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e7047	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Appreciate listeners! I have rarely heard such a thematic error and refusal of reality as did my previous speaker Philip Kucher. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] However, I would like to refer to the actual item on the agenda, namely the popular desire for vaccination: No, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, If you look at this precisely, then you realize that this is actually only half the truth, because this initiative, this popular desire, has consisted of two parts, including the direct question of the population, whether in an epidemic or an emergency policy should impose a general vaccination obligation or not. 335,000 citizens in this country have taken advantage of this direct democratic opportunity, and over 80 percent, almost 270 000 citizens, have clearly opposed the introduction of a general vaccination obligation, even in the epidemiological emergency. Now you can discuss: Is it up to the citizen to express his opinion there, or not? – We say: Yes, quite clearly, and from my point of view, politics is called upon to accept this democratic vote, because if we adopt a law here in the National Council, there are certain principles of legislation that need to be taken into account, such as: Is there any need to introduce this law at all? Is the law or measure suitable to achieve the defined objectives? Is the law possible at all? And is it proportionate? Finally, we could also talk about a certain degree of confidence and honesty in politics, but at the very end of my remarks. To the need: Is there a need for a general vaccination obligation? – There is now broad consensus, which reads: No, a general vaccination requirement is definitely not necessary. Throughout the entire corona crisis, through the various mutations and waves we have had, we have experienced twice situations where our health system was at the limit of stress, but thank God not above this limit of stress, and in the first two waves of infection there has been no vaccinations at all and practically no therapeutics at all, and in the second the vaccination has only helped very limited. However, the epidemiological situation has changed significantly with omicron: The effectiveness of the vaccination has become even less, and at the same time we – or we would have – have had much more time, in order to adapt treatment capacities, and above all we have other measures, other therapy options, medical therapy options, but of course we could also strengthen and expand other measures, such as contact tracking and isolation, in order to prevent a general duty. That's why: Was it necessary and is it necessary to adopt a general vaccination obligation? - No, I don't. Is this measure at all appropriate to achieve the objectives? Then: What was the defined objective of general vaccination? – to prevent an overload of the health system. Well, now we have had no overload of the health system without vaccination, and then we have to ask ourselves the question whether this is necessary at all. But like the expert on the last Health Committee, Dr. Dorothee von Laer, who said that even over 90 percent of all hospital patients and the deceased were over 60 years old, could be discussed at a maximum of whether a vaccination requirement for over 60 years of age is appropriate to prevent an overload of the health system. A vaccination of less than 60-year-olds is definitely not. Moreover, if we are now in efficacy, we must also say that the protective vaccinations quoted by Mr Schalmeiner against smallpox, measles and many other diseases, which were actually very large civilian, medical achievements, have a great advantage over the current preparations for protection against Covid-19: They generate sterile immunity and they prevent the transmission of the virus – and that is not possible for the current MRNA vaccines! They can't create sterile immunity, that is, they can't protect against infection and they can't protect them from transmission of the virus. Thus, this whole requirement of limiting the epidemic with these vaccines is not at present at all accessible, i.e. the suitability of the measure is not at all given. In addition: If the aim is an increase in the rate of vaccination, Mr. Federal Minister, then you or your predecessor – it has not been done under you, but under your predecessor – have reached exactly the opposite with the introduction of this general vaccination requirement, because the vaccination rate for Covid-19, the number of daily vaccinations has declined dramatically from the date the general vaccination was introduced. And not just that! It is even more objectionable to health policy that not only the number of Covid vaccinations has fallen, but also other already established vaccinations have become significantly less, Mr. Federal Minister – measles vaccinations: 50 percent decrease in the rate of vaccination in the current vaccinations; hepatitis B vaccination: over 40 percent decrease; [[Intrusion of the Abg. Loacker]] Diphtheria-tetanus vaccination: 30 percent decrease. This is the effect of this measure, and this is 100 percent contrary to your declared target. Let's get to the enforceability of the measure: If you had taken the opinions seriously in the review procedure, you would have been able to see that this measure is not really feasible. This is also one of the main reasons why this Vaccination Act has never gone beyond its phase and has not yet been strictly switched on, for there are not only constitutional concerns, but there are also major data protection concerns that the Managing Director of Elga GmbH has formulated, for example. However, there are also administrative concerns within the authorities that say: How do we manage this moloch? If the objections come, how should we deal with the whole process? There, the authorities are unhealedly overwhelmed if this is actually turned on sharp. That is, the enforceability of this law is not given at all. And last but not least, the proportionality: We are talking here of a very blatant intervention into fundamental rights, we are talking here of an intervention into the integrity of the human body – and this must be faced with a huge benefit when such strong fundamental rights intervention is taking place. And what is the benefit of this vaccination? – At best, protection against serious disease events, but facing a massive fundamental right intervention for all citizens. But it also means that we especially weigh effects and side effects, especially when we treat healthy people. It makes a difference whether I handle a cancer disease with a preparation that may also have fatal side effects, or whether I force a healthy person to deal with it. This is a serious difference, and this must be included in the considerations. For this reason alone, from my point of view, a vaccine which has only a conditional authorisation, which has no fully present safety data with a very limited efficacy, can never be administered by force or obligation. Finally, I would like to appeal about the honesty and the protection of the trust of the people. All political groups, the Federal Chancellor, the Vice-Chancellor, their predecessors as Minister of Health, all promised to the Austrian population: There will be no vaccination requirement! until this ominous political cow trade between the federal government and countries last autumn, when at once everything was different and all the parties – except the FPÖ – are tipped over and once decided on a general vaccination obligation. This is a break in the principle of trust and dishonesty, as it has not been in Austrian politics for a long time. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Stop that! Take up the Imprisonment Act and take the vote of the citizens seriously in the context of this popular desire, then you have another chance that you may get out of the top of it, otherwise the Constitutional Court will probably abolish this unwarranted law from the day on which the Imprisonment Act is strictly shut down. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e7150	Member Smolle has been reported to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e7172	Mr President. Mr. Health Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I would like to thank the proponents of popular desire for having chosen this democratic instrument in order to reinforce their concerns. I would also like to express my respect for these about 270 000 people in Austria who have expressed their opinion in this way, which is at least just over 4 percent of the electorate. If we ask ourselves how this vaccination law came about, then we need to look back briefly in this fast-moving period: At the beginning of 2020, a new virus was invaded through an immunological and otherwise unprepared world population. It was partly dramatic in some areas, which was happening there, in some countries, like us, one reacted quickly, and therefore the first wave was relatively good. However, it has progressed dramatically, and despite some preventive measures, for example, at the end of 2020, we had a phase where between 100 and 200 people in our country died with Covid every day. [[Abg. Belakovich: With Covid!]] That was a very heavy load and challenge. An exemplary international cooperation has enabled vaccines to be developed against Covid-19, primarily with the aim of preventing serious diseases and deaths, but also with the hope that this could slow down the spread of the disease, which can spread. For the original type of virus, wild type Wuhan, this has happened and also for the first variant, the British, the alpha variant. As a result, the vaccine has worked well and has also slowed infection and passed on very well. Furthermore, it was so that in the middle of last year – and that was pretty much the accepted scientific view – one came to the conclusion: For the vaccinated, the pandemic is largely over, and if enough human beings are immunized, then we get hold of it at all. It has developed differently, as we know. With Delta, at Omikron, this has not been the case anymore, but under the impression of the various prevention measures – partly involving – on the one hand, and the need for a broad immunization on the other, the idea of the vaccination law has been born. However, at the time it was already clear that the disease, the epidemiological event is a very dynamic one and therefore one has to refer to the current situation. It is especially important to note that such a preventive measure as vaccination is primarily an individual thing of the individual person. However, there are two situations where the population as a whole is concerned: This is when unprotected persons, where appropriate, overload the health care system so that it also harms the vaccinated with other diseases. And the second: if you have the opportunity to protect the vulnerable groups as a whole through wide vaccination, and this is not achieved because so many people cannot be vaccinated. – These are the two situations in which one says: It goes beyond the individual, and it becomes a social concern. Since the matter is very dynamic, because of the variants, the effectiveness of the vaccines and so on, the law has been drawn up as a framework law – that is, that is to say, it is currently to be decided quarterly: Is there any proportionality? – so it can be used, so it can be exposed because everyone was aware that with today’s knowledge one cannot prejudicate what will be the case in half a year, in a year, in two years. This balance, this proportionality depends on many factors: the susceptibility, the severity of the disease, the already existing immune protection and the further treatment possibilities – there have been some in the past year. All of this must be taken into account if, in principle, one breaks the head over whether this law should be applied or suspended. In order to comply with the law, a vaccination commission was set up. Members of the Commission regularly give opinions – and they are not alone – which are then subject to democratic control, because this must be confirmed by the National Council's main committee. Thus, we are on a good path and, considering all the possibleities, really able to decide what is the goal for the coming months and what is not. Rightly, vaccination is not currently in use. This is absolutely understandable due to the existing data situation. Finally, I would like to thank the experts in the hearing of the Health Committee, who, through their very differentiated messages, have made it possible to have a truly high-quality discussion. I would also like to thank the people in the Committee on Imprisonment, who take on an extremely responsible task, and I wish them a good and safe hand. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e9432	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. High house! Dear Seers! But above all: Dear citizens who have actively participated in the Future Conference! The idea for the future conference was announced three years ago by Commission President Ursula von der Leyen. At that time there was no mention of the many upheavals that we had to experience in the meantime, of a pandemic, a war on European soil. Nevertheless, at that time the idea was to involve citizens when it came to developing the European Union in any direction. Already at that time, it was assumed that a broad process was to be set up, that solutions to European problems and challenges should be identified, that we should together address the burning issues of the future of the European Union – and Austria was a supporter of this future conference from the very beginning. It has already been said that the conference on the future has not been a European-wide pandemic as planned. It started in May 2020, but only in 2021. However, I started this process in Austria in June 2020, because it is important to me to involve the citizens and, of course, it takes some time for this process, this conference, the ideas behind it to arrive at people. How did I do that? – I invited stakeholders to the Federal Chancellor's Office, I held future laboratories, I discussed with students, I traveled throughout the country, from Burgenland to Vorarlberg, I was on the road several times in all the federal states. Since May 2021, when the conference was actually started at European level, I can tell you that there has been a debate in Austria every second day on the future of the European Union, and, Mrs Steger, there have been 1,421 contributions from Austria, with which we are in sixth place. Where have these posts been registered? – On the multilingual platform of the European Commission. The question has been asked where one could participate; that is the answer. The further development of the European Union is a concern for Austrians. It depends on what direction we're going, and we've seen it: Whether in the community, in the club, at the main table, in the digital chat room – I also held digital speaking hours – in the school class or in the future café: It was discussed, and, ladies and gentlemen, that is the meaning behind it, that is living democracy! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs from Greens and NEOS.]] I would nevertheless like to say that we have, of course, lost time. We have lost time through the pandemic, and at European level, everything has not gone the way I and many others actually imagined it, because there were procedural questions to be resolved, there have been interinstitutional disputes, who plays what role. Yeah, that's part of it, too, but it's a shame that we lost this time, so to speak. After more than two years of work as Minister of Europe, I also have to say that the findings I am now making are quite worrying, because in particular at the upheavals we are experiencing, we see that Europe is under pressure from our European Way of Life, not to say in danger. We are experiencing an attack war, we are experiencing tensions with Russia, we are experiencing energy dependence, we see China's strivings, and we have also experienced during the pandemic that there is a shadow side of globalisation, and that is, interruption of supply chains. The good news, however, is: The strength of the European Union lies in its unity, and this unity must be demonstrated, and we are showing it at the moment. We now have to secure the European Way of Life together, and there are, of course, points to which they have already been addressed by honourable Members Lopatka: to think about the subsidiarity there, to solve the problems where they arise, and of course not to raise everything at European level, as some want to do here, and certainly to benefit from populist statements here. I would like to present to you today some ideas from this broad process, summarized in three demands that can be derived from them. The first thing we need to do is to promote the European Union's geopolitical interests, the second is to look back on the European Union's economic power, and the third concerns the European Union's sustainable strength within. – What do I mean by that? Putting Europe's own interests to the fore means being strong means playing a role in the world as well. This is an external border protection, which is clearly stated: Whoever comes to Europe can stay here only if he has an asylum ground to show. This is, of course, about the enlargement of the Western Balkans, because the Western Balkans is a matter of security for the European Union and also a question of credibility for the European Union. We have made much too many promises and we have not fulfilled them. The depression there is very, very large, and there is plenty of matchwood to trigger a fire. We now have to deliver on these promises. Clearly, in the present situation, it is not pleasant to be dependent on Russian gas, but also to be dependent on other areas, such as rare earths, when we are talking about creating the energy transition in Austria and in Europe. The second point raised, the reference to economic power, is that we need to be again leaders in Europe and want to be innovative as far as economic power is concerned. Not only do we want to see companies founded here, but we also want them to stay here, that investors invest here too, so that value creation remains here. I believe that in particular in the pandemic we have seen that it was important that Austria supported the AUA, Germany has done the same with Lufthansa in order not to become dependent on actually state-funded lines from the Asian area, but now it is about competition law also at European level into the 21st century. ==References== We should all agree, together, that we do not want to push forward any gold plating, no over-fulfilment of European directives in the national states, because this means that entrepreneurs need to look at 27 individual provisions and not find a single internal market, which is now to be completed. And, of course, we need an active immigration policy in addition to limiting illegal migration. At the moment, thousands of Russians are leaving, well-trained, their country. They are not supposed to migrate to America, we need them here in Europe. We need the immigration of highly qualified people, which we must not make difficult, just as it is at the moment. A flexible labour market would also mean that someone lives in the Salzburg Lungau and works at a Belgian company. However, this is often linked to very high bureaucratic obstacles. Taking all these points into account, which are just exemplary, we can again put Europe's economic power in the foreground, because the single market is and remains our greatest opportunity, our greatest strength. And the third point is inwards: the sustainable strengthening of the European institutions. What do I mean by that? – I think it is good and right that we have 27 EU Commissioners. Excuse me for the comment, but the challenges will not go out in the next few days to deal with it. A rule of law procedure, as we have in Article 7, will probably have to find an outcome at some point in time, and I believe that there are many other mechanisms, such as conditionality, that of linking budgetary issues with the rule of law, which are contained in the Treaties. With great pride, I would also like to say at this point that it has succeeded in Austria to have over 1,500 European Councils, which form a bridge from the European Union, which is not always easy to understand, to the community, to the citizen, where people have problems, concerns and concerns. They take over the task of passing on information, providing assistance, even when it comes to collecting funding and moving things forward in their own community. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The European Union has guaranteed freedom, peace, security, prosperity and human rights over the last decades. However, the European Union is not measured by its history. It will be measured to provide solutions to the challenges of the present and to find answers to the questions of the future. The EU – and I am quite open to you – has often remained guilty of the answers to burning questions, but it is up to us to continue developing this European Union. It is up to us to implement the things that have been presented – and I really want to appreciate every single contribution – too. That is why this is not the end of the future conference, but it is only the beginning. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e11033	Mr President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear viewer! Thank you, Mrs. Minister! The issue of security of supply is far too important to address the speeches by the colleagues of Pratergeschichten or fairy tales. I think our task here is to inform the people. One word on the topic of Minister: Mrs Geessler was here, is here and remains here. [[Congratulations on Members of the ÖVP and Greens.]] Our Federal Chancellor put it to the fore yesterday: In addition to the discharge packages already decided, it is important to create security of supply. In particular: We therefore rely on rapid and effective measures to secure our gas supply. We thus strengthen Austrian resistance and become more independent step by step. Natural gas is still absolutely needed by industry, but also for our 900 000 households. However, our goal is to ensure a level of natural gas storage of about 80 percent before the start of the winter season. Therefore, the Federal Government will also assume its responsibility and has already increased the strategic stockpiling to 20 terawatt hours in the first step, making the first step. Of course, we need several steps, so there is also the importance that we have now set up the applications on three pillars in order to secure something in the long term and ensure security of supply. Which pillars are these? – The first pillar is the Energy Steering Act. Entrepreneurship is actually a matter of course for us entrepreneurs in Austria. Entrepreneurs therefore also provide for their own gas supply and thus cover any emergencies. However, the Energy Steering Act provides that in the event of an emergency these reserves can be used. With the innovation, we also provide security of supply for our budgets, but also for legal certainty for our companies, which is very important. In the future, in the event of emergencies, we will be able to use 50 percent, but it must also be clear that this cannot happen free of charge. This innovation will therefore take place. Of course, the remaining 50 percent remains for companies to maintain the operation of their companies and thus ensure jobs. The second pillar is the introduction of a market manufacturer to ensure security of supply. In fact, security also means network stability with us. At present, the market and distribution area manager is responsible for the balancing energy and provides the balance between gas input and gas output. We now want to expand this model in which the necessary quantities are purchased or sold. The market-maker's task will be in the future, in addition to the balancing energy as well. to obtain storage space. In this way we ensure that, if a delivery stop occurs from Russia, we have sufficient compensation energy on the spot. The third pillar, which we heard yesterday in the Council of Ministers, will deal with the efficient use of storage capacity. There is the principle Use it or lose it. In addition to this, there will still be a request from our side today to ensure storage in the long term. According to the principle Use it or lose it, memory users are required, as it were, to either fill or provide their own used memory. This prevents us from a) empty gas storage and b) ensure that we also have enough storage space for the winter months. The storage of Haidach, which will be an important part of our safety concept, will also play a role in asking the minister to enter into a user agreement with Germany in order to make it clear that we also receive the rights of use. And of course we also need the network connection of the gas storage company Haidach to the Austrian network; we will do the same. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Ensuring rapid and effective security of supply for Austria is our command of the hour. ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e11192	Mrs. Leonore Geessler has now received an opinion. - Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e11213	We have in the last hour of Question Time Some of the issues have already been discussed, so I will not repeat myself now in the sense of time efficiency, but I will make a fundamental comment and two comments on some comments. We are currently really turning on many screws, on all the screws available to us to ensure security of supply in Austria, to improve security of risk, and above all to correct the mistakes of the past. We must prepare for every scenario, i.e. also for the effects of a delivery stop, because in a war situation, as we see it now, when Russia is waging a brutal attack war in Ukraine, there is a certainty that it is not possible to exclude a scenario from priority. It is precisely for this provision that there are two changes to you today, namely the Energy Control Act and the Gas Economic Act, for which I am really in favour, and if I interpret the speeches from the first round correctly, I am already looking forward to a little bit. However, I would like to explain to you what we are planning. In the Energy Control Act, it is mainly about putting large customers – that is to say, it will be primarily, I think, interesting to the real large customers in the gas area – into the position that they can provide for themselves in the event of a supply interruption and a disruption and store up accordingly gas. For this reason, this innovation is intended to protect the self-storied quantities of large consumers from volume-related steering measures as long as technically possible in the system. That's two things. This means that if an industrial company takes responsibility now and buys and stores gas itself, it should also be able to rely on these protected quantities of gas. It helps us to avoid, as long as possible, controls with high storage levels. The industry is helping to increase safety, to increase resilience. With a good compromise – 50 percent of the amount needed for production – we ensure that you have a backup, but at the same time it does not come to the horde of storage capacity. So I think we have really found a good compromise and a very, very good proposal to give industry more security in the storage of gas. The second major change in the Energy Steering Act concerns the replacement of capital disadvantages in the case of energy steering. So far, the Energy Steering Act – you know, we have never had an energy steering case in the decades of the Second Republic – has been a compensation scheme for coal and oil only. We will now propose a compensation scheme, which will go in the same direction, but also for electricity and natural gas, in order to equalise and also ensure financial security. I would now like to thank you very much, very much, for the constructive cooperation in the negotiations. There was also an agreement on the SPÖ's wish, which was still inserted and which I very much welcome, namely that this provision is still being evaluated for the replacement of capital disadvantages and is also reported to the National Council. The second part is the Gas Economic Act. In the proposed amendment to the GWG, we – as Mr Graf has already explained before – create another possibility to obtain gas on behalf of the State. We haven't had this opportunity until now. In connection with the strategic gas reserve, it is the first time that we can do this in Austria. We create another possibility here in the GWG with the so-called marketmaker. Through the balance sheet group coordinator, we can instruct energy suppliers to maintain and store natural gas as compensation energy in the gas system. That means the gas companies get a charge for the reservation, but they can use the gas themselves, if we do not then use it as a balancing energy. We are focusing on the approach in Germany. I am very closely in agreement also with my German colleague Robert Habeck, because this supply insurance is a good variant, an efficient variant in order to be able to provide short quantities of gas in the event of an emergency, and this also contributes to the goal of the Federal Government to fill the storage capacity to 80 percent by the beginning of the heating season. When it comes to gas supply, the state has access to gas reserves via supply insurance, but if the gas is not needed, the State has relatively low costs. There is also a great deal of thanks for the constructive proposals of the opposition. In this way, an amendment complements the Regulation establishing the use of the gas quantities and all other provisions of the main committee's agreement. I think that's good. We also ensure, with this measure, that we have a broad parliamentary basis for substantial expenditure and for an extensive provision that the State sets out. I know – and I also thank you for understanding – that in this exceptional situation we are doing our best to act quickly and thoroughly. I also know that I cannot always be available personally for the negotiations with Parliament, but my offer exists at any time and continues that I am also happy to be available personally for requests or for information if you consider it relevant. On the questions raised earlier in the debate: As far as gas storage is concerned, we have now actually adopted a very comprehensive package of measures, today for decision-making, or today with a request to bring the gas storage levels really to the level that we need for the next winter. This also meets the conditions that we have decided at European level once again. There is also a European obligation – it must be formally agreed by the decision-making process, but there is a provisional agreement – but with the reserve, with the extension of the reserve, with the connection of all storage to the Austrian market area, with the supply insurance, with the measures that the e-control can set, with the use-it-or-lose-it rule also for gas storage, we have now really a package that ensures that we come with better filled storage in winter. We discussed the exit scenario already in Question Time. I would just like to say one sentence: Yes, even the alternatives to Russian natural gas are fossil gas, have been obtained from countries that are not always fully developed democracies, or under environmentally damaging conditions – Mr. Kassager has pointed out. But what would be the alternative? – I do not think there is the alternative to the exit from Russian natural gas, because the alternative would be not to oppose Russia’s war of attack in Ukraine and simply to look at the incredible international crime in Butscha. That is why the consequence of the exit from Russian natural gas supplies is. I would like to thank you very much for agreeing on these two legislative proposals. Thank you very much.

This creates legal certainty for companies that are forecasting gas so that 50 percent will remain in production – but in the event of an emergency, the economy, from the public to the other 50 percent can be accessed. [[Congratulations to the ÖVP and to Mr Zorba and Mr Jakob Schwarz.]] Secondly, a marketmaker is installed. In particular, it brings security, especially for network stability, by removing or entering energy from the grid, so to speak. He can also write out memory options. That is quite, very important, so that we also have enough supplies. The third measure is to make efficient use of storage capacity. The aim of the initiative is to ensure storage in accordance with the principle of use it or lose it in the long term. That is, all those who have a storage have the opportunity to fill it themselves. If they don't, they have to offer these stores on the market. This prevents empty gas storage and thus also ensures the supply in Austria. One more word to the gas storage Haidach. In particular, it is important that we get a use agreement with Germany and that this gas storage is connected to the Austrian network so that we have access there accordingly. One thing is certain: With this federal government and with this coalition, we create security; we take the fears of the population. These measures ensure that, if a sudden stopover occurs, we have sufficient gas for the economy, but above all for all the households.

This crisis has also seriously challenged the mentality underlying a practice of just-in-time deliveries, the idea of any availability of goods at all times. Precautions and storage are once again tolerable. However, I would also like to use this debate to focus on another area and to highlight it from a different point of view, namely that of comprehensive national defence. In fact, it is about the independence of Austria, the defence of independence, and it is about putting this defence of sovereignty and independence on a broad basis, a broad basis to which everyone can contribute. Already in the mid-1970s, the concept of comprehensive national defence was aware that, in the face of complex forms of threat, a broad basis and a holistic approach to defence was needed. We are well aware of the military national defense, in which the Austrian army's soldiers do excellent work in their domestic and foreign operations. For the civil defence of the country, which is about protecting the population, but also about self-preservation, the civil protection organisations in particular are also doing great things. [[In the case of the ÖVP and the Bravoruf des Abg. Hanger.]] The economic defence of the country is also being strengthened by these legislative innovations, which are just about the stockpiles. Not least, it is also about the intellectual defence of the country, so that such threats, as we now have them, are recognized, perceived, analysed, so that preparations can be made and appropriate measures can also be taken. This sense of joint responsibility, this awareness of the shared responsibility for the security of Austria, must be strengthened and – yes – we must reinvigorate new lives in the light of the intellectual defence of the country. Ladies and gentlemen, with the decisions on these laws, we are making a very important contribution to economic defence. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e12896	Mr President. Estimated Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The common agricultural policy of the European Union or the EEC has existed since 1957. The premise was clear at the time: relatively shortly after the Second World War, sufficient food was produced for the European population – and that at the most affordable price. This agricultural policy was shaped by the first pillar, the second pillar did not exist at that time: The second pillar – almost rural development, environmental and climate policy – took place from 1999. This first pillar, this market order, basically led to two things: Firstly, food prices and agricultural goods prices were kept artificially so low that we created problems of self-sufficiency in the global south by providing the cheapest chicken cows by providing dry milk – all that Europe itself could not and could not use at that time – and at the same time support became part of producer income. That is to say, even the farmers did not really have a great deal of it. That's just the fact that's happening. At this time the farmers felt almost as much as before the farmer's exemption: They produced valuable things and on the other hand they were alms recipients. The third party was pleased, and that was actually the food industry that came at any time on cheap raw materials, no matter what origin. Austria, I think, has recognized this very soon, was actually a pioneer in the second pillar from 1999 onwards and has placed the weighting very heavily on this second pillar. I think that this second pillar is more important than ever. On this climate change, which we feel daily, and which is not only characterized by hail, storm and heavy rain, but which is perceptible daily, a small story: Last Monday I went to get my cows off the pasture in the early morning, because this is the first climate change adjustment of my farm. The cows go to pasture during the night, because even this season there are heat days over 30 degrees. When I go for the cows, I pass the grass meadows and fruit trees, which bloom beautifully and interestingly all at the same time: Apple, pear, cherry – all at the same time. Until here very idyllic and romantic, however, fact is: It's not an idiot, it's a stress flower. The trees try to grow tight, with their last strength. This stress, which the trees feel, and this heat stress that the animals feel, have equally felt my colleague Olga Voglauer and I during this CAP negotiations – with regard to the ecologicalisation of the CAP. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] It is a green success if there is again an independent bio-promotion and this is more, better taught compared to the previous programme by 40 million euros. In Austria, on average, more than two million pigs live, most of them on full-column floors. I would like to explain this to you once – and perhaps listen! – what we're talking about here. Full-column floors ‘consisting of concrete floors with inlet columns through which cot and urine can flow and collect in a pit below the columns. Thus, pigs live on full-column floors directly above their own fecals. The inevitable consequences of the ascending ammonia damps are irritable eyes and respiratory tracts. Pigs have an extremely sensitive sense of smell [...]. Also, pigs are intelligent and social animals that wander around as a favorite employment with their noses in the earth or in straw. Full-column soiling disregards all these needs. Thus, the animals in full-column farming cannot meet their natural needs in any way. [...] A 85 kg pig in this shape of position will be provided just 0.55 m2 of floor space.” The animals are so stressed that they have more common stomach ulcers than pigs that live on straw. In addition, pigs suffering from extreme stress can quickly develop harmful behaviours. This means that they bite off ears and tails of the other pigs. Ernst Gödl, Mag. Faika El-Nagashi, colleagues on a federal law that amends the Integration Act, the Recognition and Evaluation Act and the Education Documentation Act 2020 (1457 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e16737	We're on our way to the 11th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Gödl has the floor. – Ladies and gentlemen, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e16764	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Today, we are adopting the revision of three laws relating to the situation of people fleeing from Ukraine: the Integration Act, the Recognition and Evaluation Act and the Education Documentation Act. Why are we doing that? - Because we've been since 24. In February, there was a changed situation in Europe. Unfortunately, because of the war in Ukraine, very many people have been driven into flight, and as Ukraine is located in our immediate neighborhood, almost a neighbouring country of ours, it is also our very great duty to help quickly and unbureaucratically. As you know, the European Union has also reacted very quickly. It has activated the so-called mass inflow directive, which is not a very good word, but it says that, if there is a major movement of escape in Europe, special rules are to be applied in all EU countries. On the basis of this Directive and also on the basis of § 62 of our Asylum Act, the Federal Government has already adopted on 11 December. The so-called sales regulation was adopted on 1 March. The Vertriebenen regulation states that people who have to flee Ukraine are given a special status and can be very easy to stay with us. It states that temporary protection is granted without, as usual in asylum law, having a case-by-case check. There is therefore a residence permit, access to the labour market, access to the living room, access to medical care and, of course, access to educational facilities without individual checks. This is particularly important with regard to the situation of refugees from Ukraine, because, as we know, many women with their children have come to us and continue to come. At present, about 72 000 Ukrainians and Ukrainians are registered with us. Many more have come to us, many have gone further to other countries, where they may have personal links, some of which have also returned to their residential areas in Ukraine, for example in the Western Ukraine, where there is currently less or no military dispute and therefore it is possible to continue to live there. As has been said, however, 72,000 people are registered with us, and we are therefore obliged to ensure the best possible reception for them. With these legislative amendments, which we will now adopt, we want to ensure that in the area of the Integration Act, those we call displaced have access to the integration measures offered through the Integration Act through the ÖIF, the Austrian Integration Fund. On the one hand, this is about quick access to German courses and on the other hand also about access to orientation talks and orientation courses in order to make the most of integration possible. Of course, the goal is that the war will end quickly and that many Ukrainians will be able to return to their homeland as soon as possible – that is absolutely natural – but we know from the past that if war conflicts last longer, a certain part will feel at home here and will stay. It is therefore important and correct that we already offer broad integration measures. The second law, the recognition and evaluation law, is intended to help recognise professional qualifications as soon as possible. You have to imagine this: In principle, the recognition of professional qualifications must be accompanied by proof of training. But if one comes out of a war zone, if perhaps the apartment or the house is bombed down, then it can often be that these evidences are not available. There is a system for this, and this system is now also being adapted for the displaced. Thirdly, the Education Documentation Act: We want to ensure that schooling for children from Ukraine is ensured as well as quickly as possible. Overall, I believe, our goal must be to ensure the self-sustainability of those who have now arrived at us as quickly as possible. Mein Ladies and gentlemen, for us this is a question of help on the ground. Much is discussed about the extent to which asylum, asylum and the status of displaced persons are equated or to what extent they should be different. As a People's Party, we confess to what we have always demanded and said: The most important help in every conflict, in every escape movement, is the help on the spot. We're almost the neighbors. It is only one country between us and Ukraine – Hungary – the distance from border to border is a little over 400 kilometers. We are neighbors, we as Austria provide help on site. It is therefore good and right if we adapt our legal framework conditions to ensure this help on site as well. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] To conclude: Thank you very much, many institutions, but especially civil society, which has been very helpful in this phase with housing, with the support of the displaced people who have come to us, but very much thanks also to the Austrian Integration Fund. I have been in Vienna twice in the last three, four weeks and have looked at the situation on site: Its own arrival points have been set up so that registration, registration for the displaced can be as unbureaucratic as possible. – I would also like to thank you very much, Mrs Federal Minister, for the many initiatives – for example, honorary funding in this area and a Buddy Programme, a school-level support programme – so that these many people who are in a difficult situation are best supported here in Austria. I think it is good that we now adopt these laws so that the Ukrainians who have to be with us, because they have to flee, can really be taken in the best possible way.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e17151	Mrs. Susanne Raab has now spoken. - Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e17173	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Values Advisors! The war in Ukraine has dramatically changed the world of life of millions of Ukrainians. According to UNHCR, about 6.3 million Ukrainians are already on the run, and the country had to leave. Most of them are, as we know, women and children. More than 70,000 people have found refuge in Austria, so to speak, have been registered with us, which is somehow an expression of the desire to stay in our country, find refuge here and, of course, want to be found in everyday life. Of course, the sustainability perspective has not been clarified, and many Ukrainians who are with us have the desire to go back. However, we do not know how the war will develop. Europe is united in solidarity with Ukraine. This is also the legal framework that we have created for those Ukrainians who find refuge in us. We have adopted a common directive which entered into force shortly after the outbreak of the war, which, as it were, constitutes the framework for all integration measures in the individual Member States within the European Union. On the basis of these measures, we are of course setting up integration structures: As far as the labour market is concerned, we are concerned about German courses and, of course, also about educational institutions and kindergartens. We have already achieved a lot in recent weeks. We have built on the existing new structures, such as the so-called service points, in order to enable women and children unbureaucratically to organize information on how they house the children in the school, how they report to the children in the kindergarten, how they find access to the labour market, where they register for a German course, how they can find the connection to our health system. We have provided a comprehensive German course offer in all regions, which of course we have adapted to the Ukrainian needs, because many people who are coming now start with an already high level of education, often also bring German knowledge or at least English knowledge. We have expanded the funding because it is important that volunteers receive support in all their engagement in Austria, that they are taken under the arms, and that they are also promoted by the state. We have established a Buddy System that ensures throughout Austria that young people who have grown up in our country help Ukrainian young people to make leisure valuable together, because integration is also taking place in the encounter. We have done all of this, and now we want to include these structures in a legal framework, so to speak. I would like to stress that this is not really the starting point for the things that we have already implemented in the last few weeks and months, but should simply create legal certainty for Ukrainian displaced people, who have their own legal status, through appropriate laws. I would like to thank the Committee for being able to discuss the two legal changes together. On the one hand, it is the Integration Act, in which we now include the new target group of Ukrainian displaced people, so that everyone has a claim to attend a German course so that the orientation measures of the Austrian Integration Fund can all benefit. Secondly, it is also a change in the recognition and evaluation law, something that I personally feel is extremely important. There are great potentials that come with people, a great willingness to enter the labour market. The Ukrainians want to take a foothold on the labour market as soon as possible. There is also a great need for labour. Therefore, the recognition of skills brought with them is a key to enabling people to take a foothold in our country on the one hand and on the other hand to make their contribution to the labour market on the other. We will therefore ensure, by law, that, for example, qualifications which can be assessed informally because of the lack of documents which cannot be taught and demonstrated by the war flight. We will ensure that the bureaucracy is reduced to a minimum for Ukrainian displaced people, so to speak, with shorter periods of recognition. All of this is the basis of the recognition and evaluation law. Of course, I believe that you can always and everywhere improve. ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e17751	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. We're talking about prison care. I want to start with a joy or almost a surprise for me. When we discussed this issue have discussed the Committee, there was really a great deal of agreement on the need for prison care and the thanks for what is being done. I was very pleased that it could have been that different parties also have different approaches to this issue. But what everyone sees – and I believe that is something that we must and want to emphasize here in particular – is that freedom of religion as a human right also includes the exercise of religion in prisons and that we also want to uphold this human right. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] What does prison care look like in Austria? There are them in different religions or also confessions: Catholic, evangelical, Orthodox, Islamic. There are 45 pastoral caregivers in Catholic care alone. Of these, seven are mainly active, the others are secondary and voluntary. This kind of soul care is available in all prisons. When you talk to the pastoral caregivers and find out how they work and what their goals are, you hear similar words every time. I have conducted these discussions, and I mention here only some of these basic orientations and goals that the soul caregivers bring to work. One thing is that someone who comes to jail has to get ready first. This kind of help has already been remembered for this. Furthermore, over a longer period of time, the question arises again and again as to how I can deal with the events and how I can find reconciliation with myself. How can I find hope again? In addition, the preparation for the release of detention, the assistance in contacting, for example, with family members with whom one has no longer been in contact, and the resocialization. Often, the word deradization also falls, and again and again one hears that the mental care contributes to preventing suicide. When I asked a soul caregiver what the biggest challenge for her is, she told me a very deep sentence: The biggest challenge in her work as a prison soul caregiver is to be able to deal with powerlessness, because sometimes you can do nothing but simply accompany. But “just accompanying ” is so much more than“ just. ” We want to continue to ensure that this is possible in Austria. This is what our proposal is for today. I am grateful that everyone will support him. I am grateful that Mrs Minister is also concerned about supporting prison care. What remains for me is to thank the pastoral caregivers who do a very great and important job.

Point Report of the Justice Committee on the Government Template (1427, e.g._Other Organiser Protocol amending the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights in the Automatic Processing of Personal Data (1463, e.g.)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e18218	We're going to the 13th. That is the point of our agenda. There was no oral reporting. Mr Christian Drobits is the first to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-05-19-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00158_d8e18250	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Values listeners! This item on the agenda is a protocol amending the Convention on the Protection of People in the Automatic Processing of Personal Data. This protocol shows that it has long been tried, since 1988, to protect people in the processing of personal data. In the case of personal data as referred to in Article 9 GDPR, the data protection report shall clarify the specific protection measures available. The Federal Minister has clearly said that data protection as a fundamental right is a major concern for her, and this also reflects this data protection report. This data protection report, which is prepared by the Data Protection Authority, shows where there are clear errors and also violations of the protection of personal data pursuant to Article 9 GDPR are set out in this data protection report. On page 9 it is to be read that there have been 1,169 security injuries, and these security injuries concern precisely these personal data and their automatic processing. Why does this change of law need to be changed, why does it now need to amend the protocol? That is why we need them because some things have changed. In 1981, the European Data Protection Convention came into force, which ratified Austria in 1988. The EU Data Protection Framework has changed, and it is now necessary to adapt it. We also need the change because more cross-border data flow is taking place in other Contracting States. Digitalisation is progressing, and therefore it was clear to us as SPÖ in the Committee that we agreed to this Protocol to amend the Convention. In Austria, data protection in recent years has grown very much – we have a General Data Protection Regulation and a Data Protection Act, which in principle already represents this EU Data Protection Framework – but we also know very well that there are now issues such as artificial intelligence, other fields of application and, for example, algorithms in which we must be careful and sensitive. Especially in the case of the AMS algorithm, our goal must be to check carefully – and that is what the Data Protection Authority and the Administrative Court have done in the specific case – whether this is really appropriate in accordance with Article 9 GDPR. I think, Mrs Federal Minister, that although we have a good data protection right, we still have to try – as you have announced – to be prepared for these new fields of action of artificial intelligence and algorithms. However, it is also important to me to mention that the flow of data is now being regulated in Contracting States which are not EU members. Data flow and traffic with countries such as Tunisia and Mauritius are now taking over. The Convention has the great advantage that, in principle, all countries around the world can sign and ratify this Convention. At the moment, there is nothing against accepting this amendment, and in this sense I say: Thank you, Mrs Federal Minister, for the good cooperation in the field of data protection. I also thank the Data Protection Authority for the 2021 data protection report. Unfortunately, this report has not been dealt with in plenary, but it has been completed in the Committee, but it would well deserve to be dealt with here in plenary as well. – Thank you for the attention.

It protects, inter alia, the processing, i.e. any handling of personal data, whether analogous or in automated procedures. What's that supposed to mean? – It is the collection of data, it is the recording, saving of data, changing data, querying data, as well as deleting and destroying data. Ladies and gentlemen, especially the spectators, you have a right to delete and destroy your data. Please think of employee files, think of customer files too! As an Austrian Parliament, we ensure the rightful, transparent, minimally reduced and intended use of these personal data and place violations under strict and high penalties. Today's EU Convention on the Protection of People in the Automatic Data Processing of Personal Data is essentially from the 1980s, and the Protocol of amendment, which is now to be ratified by us in the Austrian Parliament and also in the other EU Member States, ensures that the Convention is adapted to technical and social changes. Ladies and gentlemen, the protection of the personal data of our citizens is a great asset for us, and we as the Austrian Parliament are strongly committed to it.

These are changes which have resulted from practice and are useful adjustments. Breit has already been discussed about the retirement in the legal order, which we think makes sense. There is now also the possibility to conduct video conferences in the everyday life of chamber activity – that is also useful. There are two things in the Notarial Order that make sense. One is that there is a clarification where a notary may operate if, at the same time, he is on a board of foundations or on the Supervisory Board of a stock company – whether he can then operate in companies that are subordinate to the foundation. That's a clarification. A really important point is – this is the last thing I want to deal with here – that there is the opportunity to do digital awards. This has been a big step which has recently been implemented, and has now been strengthened in the framework of the Corona pandemic. So far, there was only the possibility of either having a digitally signed document or a physically signed paper certificate, but not a hybrid certificate in which one party digitally signed and the other signed. This was, of course, a disadvantage in practice and, in fact, something one has seen that it would be useful if it were the possibility of these so-called hybrid certificates. This is now being implemented here, i.e. it is even more facilitated and improved to be able to carry out digital awards. So, in total, it is a sensible, practical novel, so we agree.
